for which an onsite audit has been conducted on 4th and 5th January, 2025.

ROOM FACILITY 
Under NMPB funded project for development of National Herbal Garden for 
Pharmacopoeial species, PCIM&H has established a Green House and Poly-House with cold room 
facility for germination/growing of medicinal plants from various agro-climatic zones, used in the

## Page 163

12.4.3. TRIAL CULTIVATION OF HIGH ALTITUDE, RARE AND ENDANGERED 
MEDICINAL PLANTS AT PCIM&H 
 A team of botanists of PCIM&H visited the high altitude medicine plant nurseries and 
herbal gardens maintained by High Altitude Plant Physiology Research Centre (HAPPRC), 
Srinagar, Uttarakhand at Tungnaath, Baniyakund Photibasa of Rudraprayag District. The team has 
collected saplings of rare and endangered Him alayan plants viz. Aconitum heterophyllum, 
Aconitum balfourii, Paris polyphylla, Picrorhiza kurroa, Rheum emodi, Saussurea costus, Swertia 
chirata, etc. for trial cultivation under Poly-house at PCIM&H. 

 

 

12.4.4. GO LIVE OF ONLINE PORTAL TO SELL PHARMACOPOEIAS AND SOFT 
COPIES OF PHARMACOPOEIAL MONOGRAPHS 
PCIM&H has launched an online portal to sell Pharmacopoeias and soft copies of 
Pharmacopoeial Monographs. Each monograph selling through the portal is enabled with QR code 
to check the piracy. The real-time payment is also enabled through Bharat Kosh, user can use debit 
card, credit card, net banking or UPI to make the real -time payment while purc hasing the 
monograph or pharmacopoeia. The URL of portal is https://portal.pcimh.gov.in.

## Page 172

CHAPTER 13 
PROMOTION OF INTERNATIONAL COOPERATION IN FIELD OF AYUSH 
13.1 Introduction 
The proactive efforts made by India in positioning the AYUSH (Ayurveda, Yoga & 
Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Sowa -Rigpa and Homoeopathy) Systems of Medicine as the soft 
power of India and at the same time generating evidence about the health benefits as well as 
safety of these systems has given significant boost to demand of these traditional systems of 
medicine across the world. 
The proactive efforts made by India in positioning the AYUSH (Ayurveda, Yoga & Naturopathy, 
Unani, Siddha, Sowa-Rigpa and Homoeopathy) Systems of Medicine as the soft power of India 
and at the same time generating evidence about the health benefits as well as safety of these 
systems has given significant boost to d emand of these traditional systems of medicine across 
the world. 
The Ministry developed a Central Sector Scheme for promotion of International Cooperation (IC 
Scheme) with the mandate to promote and strengthen awareness about Ayush Systems of 
Medicine; to facilitate international promotion, development and recognition of Ayush Systems 
in foreign countries; to foster interaction of stakeholders and market development of Ayush at 
international level; to support international exchange of experts and information; to give boost 
to Ayush products in International market and to establish Ayush Academic Chairs in foreign 
countries. 
13.2 Vision and Objectives of Scheme 
The scheme is meant for achieving the following objectives: 
a) To promote and strengthen awareness and interest about Ayush Systems of Medicine at 
the international level. 
b) To facilitate International promotion, development and recognition of Ayurveda, Yoga, 
Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Sowa-Rigpa and Homoeopathy; 
c) To foster interaction of stakeholders and market development of Ayush at international 
level; 
d) To support international exchange of experts and information for promotion and 
propagation of Ayush systems; 
e) To give boost to Ayush products/ services / education/ research/ training globally; 
f) To promote academics and research through establishment of Ayush Academic Chairs in 
foreign countries.

## Page 173

13.3 Brief about the activities for the promotion of Ayush globally 
Ministry of Ayush under its IC scheme undertakes various activities for the promotion of 
Ayush globally. Under this scheme, the Ministry of Ayush provides support to Indian Ayush 
Manufacturers/ Ayush Service providers to give boost to the export of Ayush products & 
services; support to Ayush experts/doctors/researchers for scientific paper presentations at 
International Level and facilitates the International promotion, development and recognition of 
Ayush system of medicine; foster interaction of stakeholders and market development of 
Ayush at international level; promote academics and research through the establishment of 
Ayush Academic Chairs in foreign countries and holding training workshop/symposiums for 
promoting and strengthening awareness and interest about Ayush Systems of Medicine at 
international level. 
As of now, the Ministry of Ayush has collaborated with more than 50 countries for the promotion 
of Ayurveda, Yoga and all other Ayush systems by virtue of signing of country to country MoU, 
research collaborations, MoU for establishing academic chairs of Ayush in foreign universities, 
setting up of Ayurveda/Ayush hospitals/academic institute, setting up of herbal garden, exchange 
of experts, deputation of Ayush experts, organizing workshops, conferences etc. Ministry of 
Ayush also have collaboration with WHO, ISO, WIPO for cooperation/under taking many 
projects in the field of Traditional Medicine. Moreover, every year 104 seats are allocated to 
foreign countries coming from different parts of the world to learn Ayurveda, Yoga , Unani etc. 
respective Ayush systems. The students are provided scholarships, tuition fees, to and fro air fare 
etc. to study Ayush systems in India. Total 75 seats have been confirmed under the scheme for 
academic year 2024-25. 
 
13.4 Memorandum of Understanding with differentCountries/Institutions/Universities: 
As of now, Ministry of Ayush signed 24 Country to Country MoUs for Cooperation in 
field of Traditional Medicine and Homoeopathy with Bangladesh, Bolivia, Brazil, China, 
Colombia, Cuba, Equatorial Guinea, Gambia, Germany, Hungary, Iran, Japan, Malaysia, 
Mauritius, Mongolia, Myanmar, N epal, Republic of Guinea, Saint Vincent & the Grenadines, 
Sao Tome & Príncipe, Suriname, Trinidad and Tobago, Turkmenistan, and Zimbabwe . Under 
these MoUs, the Ministry is conducting various activities for promotion of Ayush. 50 MoUs for 
undertaking Collaborative Research / Academic collaboration signed with foreign 
institutes/universities. 
• Establishment of Ayush Academic Chairs at foreign universities/institutes: 15 MoUs for

on the global stage. As a key facilitator, the Ministry contributes to advancing 
knowledge, research and practices through international collaboration. The WAC is a 
platform for experts, practitioners, and policymakers to engage in meaningful discussions

in the mega event.

CHAPTER 14 
OTHER CENTRAL SECTOR SCHEMES 
14.1 Ayurgyan 
14.1.1 introduction 
 The Ministry of Ayush is implementing an umbrella Central Sector Scheme namely 
“AYURGYAN” from the FY 2021-22 with the aim to support Research & Innovation in Ayush 
by providing Extra Mural Research activities and Education by providing academic activities, 
training, Capacity Building etc. 
 The Scheme has 03 components viz. (i) Capacity Building & Continuing Medical 
Education (CME) in Aysuh (ii) Research & Innovation in AYUSH from FY 2021 -22 and 3rd 
component namely Ayurveda Biology Integrated Health Research is also added under the 
scheme from this FY 2023-24. The scheme is approved upto FY 2025-26.. 
 
14.1.2 Vision and Objectives 
a) The main aim of the Scheme is to support Research & Innovation in Ayush by providing 
Extra Mural Research activities and Education by providing academic activities, training, 
Capacity Building etc. 
b) Objective 
c) A. Capacity Building and Continuing Medical Education (CME) in AYUSH 
d) The overall structure of the Scheme is aimed to encourage AYUSH personnel to 
undergo need-based professional training and bridge the knowledge gaps. 
e) Sub-component of the Capacity Building and Continuing Medical Education 
(CME) in AYUSH: 
f) Continuing Medical Education (CME) Programmes; 
g) Web-based (on-line) educational programmes; 
h) Support to organizations having domain knowledge; 
i) Two-days National Level Workshops/ Conferences for CME; and 
j) Two-day subject-/specialty CME for 50 private practitioners 
k) B. Research & Innovation in Ayush: This component has been designed to 
encourage R&D activities in priority areas so that the research findings lead to validation 
of claims and acceptability of the AYUSH approach and drugs. 
l) Objective 
m) Development of Research and Development (R & D) based Ayush Drugs for prioritized 
diseases

## Page 180

n) To generate data on safety, standardization and quality control for Ayush products and 
practices 
o) To develop evidence based support on the efficacy of Ayush drugs and therapies 
p) To encourage research on classical texts and investigate fundamental principles of Ayush 
Systems 
q) C. Ayurveda Biology Integrated Health Research (ABIHR): The main aim of this 
component is to create an ecosystem of integrative health research for convergence of 
evidence-based elements within Ayush system in sync with biomedicine, biotechnology, 
bio-physics, Nano-technology, advances in IT and such other advanced technologies for 
pragmatic integrative health practice by doing the following: 
r) To support high end integrative research with translational value for translation of Ayush 
based leads into public health; 
s) Generation of molecular, clinical and real world evidences to demonstrate safety, 
efficacy of Ayush interventions by synthesis of observations from diverse settings and 
develop protocols for tangible integrative practice; 
t) To promote competitive technology development in the field of devices and diagnostic 
tools with eminent institutions who have shared interest in Ayush, Bio -medical 
engineering, Ayur-tech and integrative biology. 
 
14.2 Major Achievements 
14.2.1 Capacity Building & CME in Ayush component 
(Capacity Building & CME in Ayush component) 
An amount of Rs.2.99 Crores was released to various institutions for conducting 36 
training Programmes for Ayush personnel. 
 
14.2.2 Research & Innovation in Ayush 
Rs.4.15 Crore was released to various institutes for conduct the 31 Research Projects 
(New/ongoing). 
 
14.2.3 Ayurveda Biology Integrated Health Research (ABIHR): 
Fund to the tune of Rs.26.12 Crore as released to support the 06 high end Research 
Projects.

## Page 181

14.3 AYURSWASTHYA Yojana 
14.3.1 Introduction 
The Ministry of Ayush is running an umbrella scheme, namely, “AYURSWASTHYA 
Yojana” from the FY 2021 -22 which was developed by merging two earlier schemes of this 
Ministry i.e., (i) Central Sector Scheme of Grant -in-aid for promotion of AYUSH intervention 
in Public Health Initiatives; and (ii) Central Sector Scheme for assistance to AYUSH 
organizations (Government/Non-Government Non-Profit) engaged in AYUSH Education/Drug 
Development & Research/ Clinical Research etc. for upgradation to Centre of Excellence (CoE). 
This scheme has been approved up to FY 2025-26. The components of the AYURSWASTHYA 
Yojana are mentioned below:- 
 
(i) AYUSH and Public Health (PHI) 
(ii) Centre of Excellence (CoE) 
 
Objectives of the PHI Component: 
a) To promote Ayush intervention for community health care. 
b) To demonstrate advantages of Ayush health care in public health. 
c) To support for implementing Sustainable Development Goal - 2 (SDG2) and Sustainable 
Development Goal -3 (SDG 3) through integrating Ayush system. 
d) Documentation of the efficacy of Ayush systems through Ayush interventions in various 
public health issues which can be taken up in larger scale for implementation in national 
health programmes. 
Objectives of the CoE Component: 
a) To support establishment of advanced/ specialized Ayush medical health unit in 
reputed Ayush and Allopathy institutions both in Government and Non-Government 
sector. 
b) To support creative and innovative proposals for establishment and up -gradation of 
both functions and facilities of reputed institutions to strengthen competencies of 
Ayush professionals in Education technology, Research & innovation, and such other 
fields necessary for promotion of Ayush at national as well as international level. 
c) To support creative and innovative proposals for prestigious organizations which 
have well -established buildings and infrastructure, and wish to work for Ayush 
systems to the level of Centre of Excellence.

## Page 182

14.3.2 Major Achievements 
(a)Major achievements of PHI Component 
Nine (09) Organizations / Institutes have been supported under the Ayush and Public 
Health (PHI) Component of AYURSWASTHYA Yojana with financial assistance of Rs.4.98 
Crore. 
(b)Major achievements of CoE Component 
An amount of Rs.24.45 Crore was released to Nine (09) Organizations for Centre of 
Excellence.

## Page 183

Chapter- 15 
Planning and Evaluation Division 
 
15.1 Introduction: 
Planning and Evaluation Division under the Ministry of Ayush is mandated to collect, 
compile and disseminate Ayush data to different Ministries /Departments. This Division collects 
data on various aspects of the Ayush system in India from various source agencies viz. AYUSH 
Directorates of ISM& Homoeopathy, State Boards of ISM& Homoeopathy, Drugs Controllers 
of ISM& Homoeopathy, Ministry of Health & Family Welfare, Directorate General of 
Commercial Intelligence & Statistics, National Institutes and Research Councils including 
Centre/State Governments. 
This Division also has a mandate for the evaluation of the Central Sector Schemes of 
the Ministry by any independent third-party agency as per the request of concerned Scheme 
PDs. 
 
15.2 Vision and Objectives: 
To provide timely, reliable and comprehensive data on Ayush systems in order to 
facilitate decision -making towards Ayush policy and schemes/programmes, monitoring, 
evaluation and recommendation for further improvement. 
To coordinate the statistical activities amongst Ministries /Departments of Govt. of 
India as well as State Governments to improve the quality of statistical data, identify gaps and 
reduce the time lag in data availability for further improvement in bringing out various Ayush 
statistical products. 
 
15.3 Activities: 
15.3.1 Publication: 
The Planning & Evaluation Division collects and compiles statistical data related to State-
wise Ayush infrastructure (Hospitals, Beds, Dispensaries etc.), Ayush Registered Practitioners, 
Undergraduate and Post-Graduate Ayush Colleges, Licensed Pharmacies, Integration of Ayush 
Systems in National Health Mission (NHM), Ayush related Foreign Trade, Outlay & 
Expenditure of the Ministry, New Initiatives, Research and Development in Ayush etc. These 
data are being published through the annual statistical publication of the Ministry of Ayush 
known as “AYUSH IN INDIA”. The publication is printed and distributed to the user end. 
15.3.2 Monitoring:

## Page 184

Planning & Evaluation Division monitors the following Plan /Sub -Plan of the Schemes 
being implemented by the Ministry of Ayush (except parliament matters i.r.o. these subjects) 
and online updates the financial and physical progress of these Plan/ Sub-Plan. 
i. Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework (OOMF) 
ii. Tribal Sub Plan (TSP) 
iii. Scheduled Caste Sub Plan (SCSP) 
iv. Monitoring of Budget allocation to North Eastern Region( NER) States under NER 
component of the schemes of the Ministry 
v. Gender Budgeting 
 
15.3.3 Other Activities: 
• National Statistical Office (NSO), Ministry of Statistics & Programme Implementation 
(MoSPI) has conducted the first survey on Ayush in the NSS 79th round during the 
period July 2022 – June 2023 on awareness and penetration of Ayush systems of 
medicine among the Indian population. The factsheet have been published and the 
major fact-finding of the survey is highlighted below: 
i. The awareness of Ayush System has reached to 96% in urban area and 94.8 % in 
rural area of India. 
ii. The percentage of households with at least one member having awareness and 
knowledge about medicinal plants/ home remedies/ LHT/ Folk Medicine is found 
to be 86.4% in urban area and 85% in rural area of India. 
iii. The use of Ayush of medicine has also reached to 52.9% in urban area and 46.3% 
in rural area of India. Among all states, Himachal Pradesh has highest percentage 
of population using Ayush system of medicine. States deep south such as 
Karnatka, Tamil Nadu, Kerala and northern states such as Ladakh, Jammu & 
Kashmir etc. are top-most users of Ayush system. 
iv. Another fact finding from the survey is that 7 out of every 10 households believe 
that Ayush medicine are effective. 
v. Out of all systems of Ayush, Ayurveda is the most commonly used system by 
people across rural and urban India for treatment. Around 45.5% urban people and 
40.5% rural people prefers Ayurveda. 
vi. Homeopathy has been used by the majority of the people in West Bengal for 
treatment of aliments.

## Page 185

vii. Yoga is a discipline which leads to unity of mind, body and soul. The survey 
estimates that at an All India level around 1.4 crore households in urban India and 
around 1.1 crore household in rural India practise yoga regularly. This shows that 
people all over India believes meditation is important for promoting overall well -
being. 
• Supply of data/information to different Ministries/Departments. 
• Uploading the data on Open Government Data (OGD) platform, India. 
• VIP/PMO references, RTI, Public Grievance, Audit Paras pertaining to the Division. 
• Matters relating to Parliament Question/Assurance. 
• Other miscellaneous work pertaining to the Division.

CHAPTER 16 
PROGRESSIVE USE OF HINDI 
16.1 Implementation of Official Language Hindi 
In order to ensure compliance with the constitutional and statutory provisions regarding 
the official language of the union and to promote the progressive use of Official Language Hindi 
for official purpose of the Ministry of AYUSH, the Hindi Section of this Ministry has been 
making all out efforts to achieve the annual targets set by the Department of Official Language, 
Ministry of Home Affairs. In this effort, apart from the Hindi translation of documents related to 
Section 3(3) of the Official Languages Act, 1963 and other documents required for the 
progressive use of Hindi in day to day official works, the Hindi Section has also undertaken 
various important activities during the period from 01 January, 2024 to 31 December, 2024 
which are given below: 
 
1. The Ministry held its Official Language Implementation Committee meetings under the 
chairmanship of Joint Secretary/Deputy Director General/Advisor, to assess the progressive use 
of Official Language Hindi and to gradually increase its uses. 
2. Further this year also, Hindi Section nominated the employees working in the Ministry for 
Hindi Shorthand Training Programme and for training in ‘Prabodh’ and ‘Pragya’ courses. 
3. The meeting of the Official Language Implementation Committee held in Kautilya Auditorium 
of the Ministry of AYUSH was chaired by Dr. M.A. Qasmi, Advisor (U). In the meeting, the 
Ministry's in -house magazine ‘Apratim AYUSH’ dedicated to Official Language Hindi was 
released. Smt. Purnima Bose, Deputy Director (O.L.), Shri Anil Kumar, Assistant Director 
(O.L.), Shri Sanjeev Kumar, Under Secretary, Smt. Sheela Tirkey, Under Secretary and Shri 
Akhilesh Motawala, Section Officer etc. were present in the meeting.

## Page 198

CHAPTER 18 
AYUSH GRID 
18.1 Introduction 
Ministry of Ayush has envisaged Ayush Grid project to be the IT backbone of entire 
Ayush sector allowing integration of all stakeholders (including all the entities under the 
Ministry) and functions/ services related to Ayush sector. From a user's perspective, the Ayush 
Grid shall be an integration of IT projects of all streams of Ayush viz. Ayurveda, Yoga & 
Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, Sowa Rigpa and Homeopathy in their functional areas. It is a six 
years project with one year of development phase and five years of O& M Phase. Through four 
distinct layers —the Core layer, National Layer, State Layer, and Citizen access layer the 
components of the Ayush grid cover all Ayush Sector's verticals. 
 
18.2 Major Achievements 
18.2.1 In 2024, the work of development of Ayush Grid project has been awarded to BISAG-
N and accordingly, Ministry of Ayush has commenced the digitization and revamping of all 
prominent Ayush assets to modernize and enhance their functionality. This initiative aligns 
with the ministry's vision of leveraging technology to promote and propagate Ayush systems 
of healthcare effectively and Ayush Grid shall act as the IT backbone of the entire Ayush 
Sector. 
18.2.2 Key Enhancements and smooth functioning of eLMS and AHMIS : Ministry of 
Ayush launched the e-Learning Management System (eLMS) and the Comprehensive Ayush 
Hospital Management Information System (AHMIS) in 2023. These initiatives aimed to 
streamline educational processes for students and enhance healthcare management through 
digital solutions respectively. In 2024, both platforms have demonstrated significan t 
operational success handling a large number of registrations and users efficiently. In the eLMS, 
customizations and design modifications have been made throughout the year to address user 
feedback, enhance navigation and optimize the system for diverse learning needs. Similarly, 
AHMIS, currently deployed in over 100 Ayush facilities, which provides an upgraded 
Electronic Health Records (EHR) system for healthcare providers, has been continuously 
improved. Design and security modifications were implemented to ensure seamless operation 
for doctors and patients. A key enhance ment in 2024 is the integ ration of AHMIS with the 
Ayushman Bharat Digital Mission (ABDM) allowing the system to work with the Ayushman 
Bharat Health Account (ABHA ID). This integration has streamlined patient identification and

## Page 199

medical record management improving overall efficiency and the user experience for 
healthcare professionals and patients. 
18.2.3 Ayush Asset Mapping on the PM GatiShakti Portal: The PM GatiShakti 
National Master Plan (PMGS -NMP) was launched on 13th October 2021 to provide 
multimodal connectivity infrastructure for various economic zones. The Cabinet Committee 
on Economic Affairs (CCEA) a pproved the implementation of PMGS -NMP on 21st October 
2021. As part of integrating social sector ministries onto the portal, the Ministry of Ayush was 
onboarded onto the PM GatiShakti Portal in 2023. Subsequently, comprehensive asset mapping 
using the PM GatiShakti-Ayush Asset Mapping Tool (PGAAMT) began in 2024. In 2024, the 
Ministry of Ayush successfully commenced asset mapping using the PGAAMT developed by 
BISAG-N in coordination with DPIIT. This digital tool facilitated the precise capturing of 
geographical locations and details of Ayush assets under six key layers: 
1. Ayush Health Facilities 
2. Ayush Educational Institutes 
3. Ayush Research Centers 
4. Medicinal Plant Facilities 
5. Ayush Drugs and Food Materials Facilities 
6. Ayush Heritage 
To ensure the accuracy and reliability of the data, a hierarchical verification system was 
implemented across three levels: 
• Maker: Responsible for initial data entry and collection. 
• Checker: Verifies the accuracy and completeness of the data entered. 
• Approver: Provides final approval for the data to be integrated into the portal. 
This systematic approach ensured the creation of a robust and error -free database of Ayush 
assets. The integration of validated and verified data into the National Master Plan portal also 
began. Ministry of Ayush updated its data periodically through secure user identification 
credentials. The Logistics Division, DPIIT, and BISAG -N provided continuous technical 
assistance to ensure the smooth creation, updating and integration of data layers using APIs. 
18.2.4 Ayush Grid cell actively contributed in the WHO technical meeting on 
finalization of the ICD -11 TM2 Module release version held on 25 -26 November, 2024 and 
International Conference on Traditional and Complementary Medicine (INTRACOM) on 27 -
28 November 2024 in Shah Alam, Malaysia.

## Page 200

18.2.5 The Digital Health Initiatives of the Ministry of Ayush and Ayush Grid were 
showcased at World Ayurveda Congress (WAC) and Arogya Expo, Dehradun, Uttarakhand on 
13-14 December 2024. 
18.2.6 Facilitating and leading the development of Benchmark
 policy document for the use of Artificial Intelligence in Traditional Medicine: The 
Ministry of Ayush has supported the development of the Benchmarking of the utilization of AI 
in traditional medicine by acting as a topic driver for Traditional Medicine as part of the Focus 
Group on Artificial Intelligence for Health (FG -AI4H). In continuation of this ini tiative, the 
Ministry has participated in the Global technical consultation for mapping the a pplication of 
artificial intelligence in TM, 11th, 12th September 2024 at All India Institute of Ayurveda 
(AIIA), New Delhi, wherein discussion was undertaken on the draft “Mapping the Application 
of Artificial Intelligence in Traditional Medicine, Technic al Brief” prepared by WHO team. 
The focus group initiative has now been upgraded as the “Global Initiative on AI for Health 
(GI-AI4H)” which is led by WHO, ITU, WIPO. The Ministry is working with the GI -AI4H 
Secretariate for finalizing the ToR for material izing the work on the development of 
“Benchmarks for use of AI for Traditional Medicine”. 
18.2.7 Launch of the Holistic Portal: In 2024, the Holistic Portal has been made 
operational to serve as a central hub for hosting Ayush-related activities and campaigns. This 
portal consolidates various initiatives such as quiz competitions, essay writing contests and 
awareness programs, particularly for events like the International Day of Yoga and Ayurveda 
Day. By leveraging digital media platforms, the Holistic Portal aims to streamline engagement 
and enhance outreach efforts for the ministry's activities. 
18.2.8 Revamp of the Ministry Website: On 20th August 2024, the Ministry of Ayush, 
in partnership with NIC, revamped its official website to reflect its decade- long journey since 
its inception on 9th November 2014. The upgraded website highlights ministry's commitment 
to reviving ancient systems of medicine and advancing Ayush healthcare. The updated platform 
provides enhanced functionality and accessibility showcasing the ministry's achievements and 
ongoing efforts in promoting holistic healthcare. 
18.2.9 Key Enhancements and smooth functioning of Ayush NGO Portal: In 2024, the 
Ayush NGO Portal has been upgraded, enhancing security and streamlining processes for all 
Central Sector Schemes. The portal is now integrated with NGO Darpan under NITI Aayog 
and PFMS under the Ministry of Finance, ensuring end -to-end digitalization from proposa l 
submission to grant disbursement, promoting transparency and efficiency.

## Page 201

18.2.10 Integration of ICD -11 API into NAMASTE Portal The NAMASTE Portal, 
launched during Ayurveda Day in 2017, has been further enhanced in 2024 with the integration 
of the ICD -11 for Mortality and Morbidity Statistics API and an embedded coding tool. 
Maintained by WHO, the International Classification of Diseases (ICD) is vital for collecting 
primary and secondary data on communicable and non - communicable diseases, as well as 
mortality statistics. This integration ensures standardized data collection an d improved 
interoperability, supporting evidence- based policymaking and research. 
18.2.11 Revamp of BIMSTEC TM Portal: The BIMSTEC Traditional Medicine (TM) 
Portal was revamped in 2024 and is now hosted under Ayush Grid enhancing its functionality 
and accessibility for users across member countries. 
18.2.12 Security audit of portals : A comprehensive security audit of 11 Ayush portals 
has been initiated to safeguard critical digital assets from vulnerabilities and mitigate the risks 
of cyber threats. 
18.3 Cybersecurity measures taken by Ministry of Ayush to minimize cyber threats: 
Ministry has adopted a number of comprehensive cyber security measures to identify and 
mitigate risks to safeguard against potential threats. Below are the key cyber security measures 
are in place: 
18.3.1 SSL (secure socket layer) are implemented across all web application to ensures 
that sensitive information remains protected from unauthorized access or interception. 
18.3.2 Security Audit of all websites hosted by Ministry of Ayush, so that websites can 
be secured with all kind of known vulnerabilities. 
18.3.3 Regular vulnerability assessments are conducted to identify and address 
potential weaknesses in Ministry's IT infrastructure. Recently as per the orders from Cabinet 
Secretary, a comprehensive infrastructure audit of all digital assets has been carried out to 
improve the security posture of Ministry of Ayush. 
18.3.4 Access Control and Authentication Mechanisms are rigorously enforced across 
all digital platforms with the Ministry. Multi-factor authentication (MFA) is also implemented 
to add an extra layer of security. 
18.3.5 Regular training sessions and awareness programs are conducted to educate 
staff members about best practices for data protection, phishing awareness, cyber arrest and 
cybersecurity hygiene. In accordance with our commitment to maintaining a secure 
environment, a comprehensive list of do's and don'ts pertaining to Cyber security has been 
prominently displayed at every entrance of sections within our ministry premises, serving as a 
professional reminder of best practices and guidelines for all personnel.

## Page 202

18.3.6 Incident Response and Disaster Recovery Planning are in place. In the event of 
a security incident or breach, Cyber Crises Management Plan (CCMP) has been prepared. This 
plan provides clear protocols for containing and mitigating the impact of security inc idents 
along with incidence reporting to CERT-In, roles and responsibilities of officers at the time of 
incidence and continuation of business of Ministry of Ayush to serve the citizens of the country. 
18.3.7 Preparation of inventory of all digital assets of Ministry of Ayush along with 
the dates of end of life, end of support details. 
18.3.8 End Point Detection and Response (EDR) solutions have been deployed across 
the Ministry's network endpoints to boost ability to detect, investigate, and respond to potential 
security threats in real -time. Implementation of Unified Endpoints Management (UEM ) for 
monitoring and compliance of cyber security policies. 
18.3.9 Implementation of host firewall policy on all endpoints to secure all endpoints 
with any kind of external or internal treats. 
By implementing these digital security measures, the ministry is committed to maintain, secure 
and resilient digital environment, safeguarding sensitive information and fulfilling our 
responsibilities to stakeholders and the public.
